Um... no, not really. I mean, I definitely feel tired sometimes, but I think that's just normal, right? Like, I'm a manager, so I work pretty long hours, and I try to keep up with chores around the house so my wife doesn't have to do everything.

Um... no, I wouldn't really say I'm sad. I mean, sure, I have moments where I feel a little down, but who doesn't, right? I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially at work. My team needs me to be steady, and I don't want to bring any negative energy home to my wife, either.

Um... well... I guess I wouldn't say "numb," exactly. That sounds so... cold, you know? But... I suppose things do feel a little different lately. 

My wife, she's so great, she always tries to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routines—like we used to make breakfast together and just relax—and I go along with it, of course, because I want to make her happy. But... I don't know, I just don't feel that real happiness or... or closeness anymore. Even at work, when something goes really well and the team hits a big goal, I just sort of... register it, I guess. I don't get that same spark of satisfaction I used to. 

I try not to let it show, though. I don't want my wife to think she's doing something wrong, or my team to think I'm not proud of them. I still smile and say all the right things. I just... I guess I just feel a little disconnected from the good feelings right now. Is that... is that something that happens when people get really stressed out? I'm trying really hard to get back to my normal self, I promise.

Oh, no... no, definitely not completely. That sounds so... well, it sounds terrible, doesn't it? Like I'm just totally giving up, and I'm definitely not doing that. 

I mean, I still love being around her. When she laughs at something on TV, or when we finish a project around the house, I still feel... glad. You know? I'm glad she's happy. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah, I guess... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it is less than it used to be. I mean, like I said, I still care, and I want to be happy... but the actual feeling of it, the real happiness or that sense of closeness with my wife during our Sunday routines... it's just not quite there the way it was. 

And it's the same at work, too. When a project goes well, I know I should feel that satisfaction, and I act like I do for the team's sake, but inside it just feels a bit flat. I feel terrible even admitting that. It makes me sound ungrateful, doesn't it? I really don't want to be ungrateful. I have a good life. I'm just... struggling to feel the good parts of it right now. Is that... is that normal for someone dealing with a lot of stress? I just want to make sure I'm not doing something wrong here.

Is that the kind of timeline you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest or pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy, aside from just feeling emotionally distant?\n\nPatient: Um... well, honestly, I'm not really sure if I'd say I've lost interest in things. I still try to do the things I used to do, you know? Like, I still go out to the garage and tinker with my tools when I have time, and I still watch the game on the weekends. \n\nI guess I just... I don't feel that same spark of excitement I used to, but I'm still doing them. I'm trying to keep up with everything, I really am. I don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around doing nothing, because I'm definitely staying busy. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining that okay. I just wouldn't say I've stopped doing things I enjoy, it's just... maybe the feeling is a little different right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or have a lack of energy most days, even when you haven’t done anything physically or mentally exhausting?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I definitely feel tired sometimes, but I think that's just normal, right? Like, I'm a manager, so I work pretty long hours, and I try to keep up with chores around the house so my wife doesn't have to do everything. \n\nI wouldn't say I'm dragging or lacking energy most days, especially not if I haven't done anything. I try to stay really active and keep pushing through, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just complains about being tired all the time when there's work to be done. I usually have enough energy to get through the day. Does that sound normal for someone my age? I hope I'm not making it seem like I'm slacking off or anything.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have a persistently low or sad mood most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't really say I'm sad. I mean, sure, I have moments where I feel a little down, but who doesn't, right? I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially at work. My team needs me to be steady, and I don't want to bring any negative energy home to my wife, either. \n\nSo, no, definitely not most of the day. I just try to keep a smile on and push through whatever's going on. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I just really don't want to be one of those people who complains or wallows, you know? I try to stay as upbeat as I can. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you’re not living up to your own or others’ expectations, even when there’s no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I try really hard to do a good job at work and be a good husband, and I think I do okay with that. I don't really sit around feeling guilty about things. \n\nI mean, sure, everyone makes mistakes sometimes, and I always try to take responsibility when I do, but I don't think I beat myself up over it or anything. I just try to fix it and move on. I really want to make sure I'm pulling my weight and meeting everyone's expectations, you know? I hope I'm doing a good enough job at that. I try my best not to let anyone down. I wouldn't want my team to think I'm slipping or anything. \n\nSometimes, you know, if it's a really busy day, my mind might wander for a second, but I think that happens to everyone, right? I just try to quickly snap back to it and get the job done. I really pride myself on being sharp and reliable. I hope that's what you're looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. I don't want to be a bother or make her worry that I'm not eating her cooking. \n\nI think my weight has been pretty steady. I try to keep myself in decent shape, you know? I don't want to let myself go. Sometimes I might skip lunch if we're really slammed at the office, but I always make up for it later. I think that's pretty normal for someone with a busy schedule, right? I hope I'm doing okay with that. I really try to take care of myself so I can keep taking care of everything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual or, on the other hand, feeling restless or fidgety, almost like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I try to keep a pretty steady pace, really. I mean, being a manager, you sort of have to set the tone for the rest of the team, so I always make sure I look composed. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm dragging my feet or, you know, acting all jittery and making everyone else nervous. \n\nI guess sometimes I might tap my pen during a long meeting, but... that's just a normal habit, isn't it? I think everyone does that a little bit when they're thinking. I really try to just stay focused and keep things running smoothly. Am I answering these okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um... no. No, definitely not. My team needs me to be steady, and I don't want to bring any negative energy home to my wife, either. \n\nSo, no, definitely not most of the day. I just try to keep a smile on and push through whatever's going on. I hope that's the right way to handle it? I just really don't want to be one of those people who complains or wallows, you know? I try to stay as upbeat as I can. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you’re not living up to your own or others’ expectations, even when there’s no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I try really hard to do a good job at work and be a good husband, and I think I do okay with that. I don't really sit around feeling guilty about things. \n\nI mean, sure, everyone makes mistakes sometimes, and I always try to take responsibility when I do, but I don't think I beat myself up over it or anything. I just try to fix it and move on. I really want to make sure I'm pulling my weight and meeting everyone's expectations, you know? I hope I'm doing a good enough job at that. I try my best not to let anyone down. Does that sound like a healthy way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to focus, make decisions, or think clearly lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I think my focus is pretty good, actually. I mean, at work, I have to make a lot of decisions every day, and I always try to make sure I'm on top of things. I wouldn't want my team to think I'm slipping or anything. \n\nSometimes, you know, if it's a really busy day, my mind might wander for a second, but I think that happens to everyone, right? I just try to quickly snap back to it and get the job done. I really pride myself on being sharp and reliable. I hope that's what you're looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. I don't think I've had any real trouble with thinking clearly or making choices.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my wife makes sure we eat pretty healthy at home, and I always try to make sure I'm sitting down for dinner with her. I don't want to be a bother or make her worry that I'm not eating her cooking. \n\nI think my weight has been pretty steady. I try to keep myself in decent shape, you know? I don't want to let myself go. Sometimes I might skip lunch if we're really slammed at the office, but I always make up for it later. I think that's pretty normal for someone with a busy schedule, right? I hope I'm doing okay with that. I really try to take care of myself so I can keep taking care of everything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been moving more slowly than usual or, on the other hand, feeling restless or fidgety, almost like you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. I try to keep a pretty steady pace, really. I mean, being a manager, you sort of have to set the tone for the rest of the team, so I always make sure I look composed. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm dragging my feet or, you know, acting all jittery and making everyone else nervous. \n\nI guess sometimes I might tap my pen during a long meeting, but... that's just a normal habit, isn't it?
